# germany-transfer-pricing: figure and rule ledger

Guide: [germany-transfer-pricing](../../skills/germany/germany-transfer-pricing.md). Accepted Guide SHA-256: `e0fb36c2e05112e253ac55ce2bd1079fe537dfaac9638c662e7f7fca68fe5783`.
Repackaged from the completed [independent country review](https://github.com/openaccountants/openaccountants/blob/bceaf70083a8c8573ba8558a339ab11b1b4095bf/ledgers/germany-2026-evidence/independent-review/report.md) at `bceaf70083a8c8573ba8558a339ab11b1b4095bf`. The Guide bytes, all current ledger claims and all original-topic destinations are preserved. This is a packaging change; it does not claim a new legal review or accountant attestation.
Original published ledger SHA-256: `b6b30b59aeff0c1dabcbb20008dc06a241b968ac2680a623f46b2c96f8ac19f2`. Full source captures, earlier versions, scripts and review chains remain in the original archive; none is added by this replacement. Source excerpts below retain their actual read dates. Short excerpts identify the previously reviewed operative unit; they do not replace its complete conditions.

| 001 | Transfer pricing in Germany (Verrechnungspreise) LF[19, 19] | How Germany prices and documents dealings between a business and its related parties abroad: the arm's length rule, the records a business must keep, the master file, the country-by-country report, what must be handed over in a tax audit and when, the surcharges, and advance pricing procedures. It is for German companies, partnerships and permanent establishments that deal with related parties abroad, and for their advisers. Figures are for tax year 2026. The statute figures are read from the consolidated federal law pages as they stood in September 2026. Four finance ministry papers are also used, each dated another year: the administrative principles on transfer pricing of 12 December 2024, the information sheet on the transaction matrix of 2 April 2025, the application decree on advance pricing procedures of 26 June 2024, and the administrative principles on documentation duties of 3 December 2020. The 2020 paper is older than the changes that apply from 1 January 2025. This Guide uses it only for points that the later law did not change, and names it each time. | [TP.date24](#q-f59031db517cc8), [TP.date20](#q-58538e9253ea37), [TP.datematrix](#q-72452492a58a8b), [TP.dateapa](#q-63b46f32d6fcf3), [TP.eg37-5](#q-ccf3292d0e0e6b), [TP.b24-doc](#q-036601a8d84791). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 009 | Section 1: Quick Reference LF[35, 35] | \| Who is a related party \| A holding of at least one quarter, direct or indirect, in capital, membership rights, participation rights, voting rights or company assets; a claim to at least one quarter of the profit or of the liquidation proceeds; controlling influence, direct or indirect; a third person who stands in one of these positions to both sides; or influence that comes from outside the business relation, or an own interest in the other side earning the income (§ 1(2) AStG). The law prints the share in words \| | [TP.a1-2](#q-19d9214022907c). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 010 | Section 1: Quick Reference LF[36, 36] | \| Which dealings are covered \| Business relations with a related party abroad, and dealings between a business and its permanent establishment in another state (§ 1(1), (4) and (5) AStG). A partnership is itself a taxpayer under this rule. Not covered: dealings that rest on an agreement under company law, meaning one that directly changes the legal position as a shareholder (§ 1(4) sentence 1 no. 1 b AStG) \| | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe), [TP.a1-4](#q-4764398d0b96aa), [TP.a1-5](#q-b0f5433a764e8f). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 011 | Section 1: Quick Reference LF[37, 37] | \| Direction of the rule \| § 1 AStG applies where income was reduced by terms that are not at arm's length. § 1(1) AStG names the case where income was reduced ("gemindert"). For permanent establishments § 1(5) AStG names two cases: the German income of a taxpayer with limited tax liability is reduced, or the foreign income of a taxpayer with unlimited tax liability is raised \| | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe), [TP.a1-5](#q-b0f5433a764e8f). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 018 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[48, 48] | - **Who must keep records.** A taxpayer must keep records on the kind and content of its business relations within § 1(4) AStG. See § 90(3) AO at https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/ao_1977/__90.html The rule that the records serve, § 1 AStG, is about business relations abroad. The ministry describes the transaction matrix as a table of the taxpayer's cross-border business relations with related parties and permanent establishments (information sheet of 2 April 2025, part I). | [TP.ao90-3](#q-5a53aa44c8c662), [TP.matrix-def](#q-7b4bdc42170029). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 019 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[49, 49] | - **No size limit for the duty itself.** § 90(3) AO prints no minimum amount. Smaller businesses get a lighter way to meet the duty, not a release from it. See 2.2. | [TP.ao90-3](#q-5a53aa44c8c662), [TP.g6-1](#q-8d08eabd92bca2). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 020 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[50, 50] | - **Three parts.** The records cover (1) an overview of the transactions (Transaktionsmatrix), (2) a presentation of the transactions (Sachverhaltsdokumentation) and (3) the economic and legal basis for arm's length terms, with the time the price was set, the method used and the comparables used (Angemessenheitsdokumentation). | [TP.ao90-3](#q-5a53aa44c8c662). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 021 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[51, 51] | - **A fourth part for larger group companies.** The master file. See 2.1. | [TP.ao90-3](#q-5a53aa44c8c662). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 022 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[52, 52] | - **The standard.** The records must show a serious effort to deal at arm's length. An expert third party must be able to see, within a reasonable time, what the taxpayer did and whether it kept to the arm's length rule. See § 2(1) GAufzV at https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/gaufzv_2017/__2.html Records that are unusable in essence count as not made. See § 1(1) GAufzV at https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/gaufzv_2017/__1.html | [TP.g2-1](#q-164e0e6d51aceb), [TP.g1-1](#q-95ed9425e0687a). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 023 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[53, 53] | - **One method is enough.** The taxpayer does not have to keep records for more than one suitable method (§ 2(2) GAufzV). | [TP.g2-2](#q-291ad250f55b96). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 024 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[54, 54] | - **Per transaction, with grouping allowed.** Records are made per transaction. Transactions that are comparable in functions and risks may be grouped, if the grouping follows rules set in advance and the transactions are alike, or if grouping is usual between third parties. Group transfer pricing guidelines that are in fact followed can replace records per transaction (§ 2(3) GAufzV). | [TP.g2-3](#q-9be52c367dd131). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 025 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[55, 55] | - **Long-running arrangements.** If circumstances change in a way that matters for the price, the taxpayer must go on collecting information after the deal is closed. This applies above all where tax losses show up that a third party would not have accepted (§ 2(4) GAufzV). | [TP.g2-4](#q-88c7873f2383e9). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 026 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[56, 56] | - **Language.** Records are made in German. The tax office can allow exceptions on application. The application can be made before the records are written. It must be made at the latest without delay after the tax office asks for the records (§ 2(5) GAufzV). The ministry's 2020 paper adds that another living language, English above all, can be allowed if the records can still be understood in reasonable time, that this applies to the master file in particular, and that translations asked for later must then be supplied in reasonable time (no. 34). | [TP.g2-5](#q-cb2e34c255b412), [TP.b20-34](#q-1a49bea9a89438). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 027 | Section 2: Documentation Requirements LF[57, 57] | - **Database searches.** A taxpayer that uses databases must disclose the search strategy, the search criteria, the result and the further selection steps. The whole search must be open to checking at the time of the audit. See § 4(3) GAufzV at https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/gaufzv_2017/__4.html | [TP.g4-3](#q-f2eb4bf3027748). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

The ministry's 2020 paper puts it this way: the duty to present records can also be met by giving information orally in time and presenting documents that already exist (no. 38). | [TP.g6-1](#q-8d08eabd92bca2), [TP.b20-38](#q-387bdbc9d7a8b5), [TP.ao90-4](#q-d5ffb8feb121c7). | 106 | 2.4 Country-by-Country Report (CbCR) LF[173, 173] | \| Appointed company \| A foreign group parent can appoint an included German group company to file for the group. That company then files with the BZSt (§ 138a(3) AO) \| | [TP.cb-3](#q-4be60685c3a0f5). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 107 | 2.4 Country-by-Country Report (CbCR) LF[174, 175] | \| Fallback \| If the BZSt has not received the report of a group with a foreign parent, an included German group company must as a rule file it. When one German company files, the others are free. A company that cannot get or make the report must tell the BZSt within the filing period and give all the data it has or can get (§ 138a(4) AO). The same duties apply to the German permanent establishment of a foreign business that is included in consolidated accounts as foreign group parent or as included foreign group company (§ 138a(4) sentence 5 AO) If that company could reasonably expect timely transmission and later learns that transmission failed without its fault, it must fulfil the report duty or the unavailable-report notice/data duty within one month after learning of the non-transmission (§ 138a(4) sentence 4 AO). This exception also extends to the specified German permanent establishment under sentence 5; it does not excuse a culpable failure. \|<br>\| Filing deadline \| Ordinarily at the latest one year after the end of the financial year that the report covers. For the justified-expectation, no-fault fallback in § 138a(4) sentence 4 AO, the deadline is instead one month after learning of the non-transmission, including the specified German permanent establishment (§ 138a(6) AO). It is not another twelve-month period. \| | [TP.cb-4](#q-81697d8f14aa5a), [R61.cb-4](#q-6869010debd45a), [R61.cb-6](#q-c4b436e8376a68). R61 bounded source-backed condition successor |
| 108 | 2.4 Country-by-Country Report (CbCR) LF[174, 175] | \| Fallback \| If the BZSt has not received the report of a group with a foreign parent, an included German group company must as a rule file it. When one German company files, the others are free. A company that cannot get or make the report must tell the BZSt within the filing period and give all the data it has or can get (§ 138a(4) AO). The same duties apply to the German permanent establishment of a foreign business that is included in consolidated accounts as foreign group parent or as included foreign group company (§ 138a(4) sentence 5 AO) If that company could reasonably expect timely transmission and later learns that transmission failed without its fault, it must fulfil the report duty or the unavailable-report notice/data duty within one month after learning of the non-transmission (§ 138a(4) sentence 4 AO). This exception also extends to the specified German permanent establishment under sentence 5; it does not excuse a culpable failure. \|<br>\| Filing deadline \| Ordinarily at the latest one year after the end of the financial year that the report covers. For the justified-expectation, no-fault fallback in § 138a(4) sentence 4 AO, the deadline is instead one month after learning of the non-transmission, including the specified German permanent establishment (§ 138a(6) AO). It is not another twelve-month period. \| | [TP.cb-6](#q-4a39a3795efb25), [R61.cb-4](#q-6869010debd45a), [R61.cb-6](#q-c4b436e8376a68). R61 bounded source-backed condition successor |

| 110 | 2.4 Country-by-Country Report (CbCR) LF[177, 177] | \| Notification \| There is no separate yearly notice to the BZSt. A German business states in its tax return whether it is a German group parent, an appointed company, or an included German company of a group with a foreign parent. In the last case it also states which business files the report and with which authority. If that statement is missing, the included German company must itself file the report on time (§ 138a(5) AO). The same applies to such a German permanent establishment (§ 138a(5) sentence 4 AO). The BZSt's questions and answers page says the BZSt need not be told in advance which group company will file: https://www.bzst.de/DE/Unternehmen/Intern_Informationsaustausch/CountryByCountryReporting/FAQ/FAQ_node.html Its page on tax return statements says the corporate tax return takes these entries in Anlage WA: https://www.bzst.de/DE/Unternehmen/Intern_Informationsaustausch/CountryByCountryReporting/Steuererklaerungen/steuererklaerungen_node.html \| | [TP.cb-5](#q-11d1e9f8f12de5), [TP.faqnotice](#q-d1c9a56c00092c), [TP.taxreturn](#q-d3a63871e1e204). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 113 | 3.1 Definition LF[185, 185] | - **Arm's length principle.** Where a taxpayer's income from a business relation abroad with a related party is reduced because the terms, prices above all, differ from what independent third parties would have agreed in the same or similar circumstances, the income is set as it would have been under terms agreed between independent third parties. See § 1(1) AStG at https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/astg/__1.html | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 114 | 3.1 Definition LF[186, 186] | - **What the third parties are assumed to know.** They know all essential circumstances of the business relation and act as prudent and diligent managers (§ 1(1) sentence 3 AStG). | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 115 | 3.1 Definition LF[187, 187] | - **Facts first.** The actual circumstances of the transaction decide. The law asks for a function and risk analysis (who performs which functions, bears which risks, uses which assets) and a comparability analysis. The circumstances at the time the transaction was agreed count (§ 1(3) AStG). | [TP.a1-3](#q-1a355ec9874fd1). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 116 | 3.1 Definition LF[188, 188] | - **Stacking.** If the arm's length rule leads to a larger correction than the other rules, the larger correction is made on top of them (§ 1(1) sentence 4 AStG). As other rules the 2024 administrative principles name, for companies, the hidden profit distribution and the hidden contribution, and for sole traders and partnerships, contributions and withdrawals (no. 1.1). | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe), [TP.b24-1.1](#q-773ded6abdbee5). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 117 | 3.1 Definition LF[189, 189] | - **Hypothetical test.** Where no comparable values can be found, a hypothetical arm's length test is made from the view of both the provider and the recipient, using economically recognised valuation methods (§ 1(3) sentence 7 AStG). | [TP.a1-3](#q-1a355ec9874fd1). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 118 | 3.1 Definition LF[190, 190] | - **No agreement under the law of obligations.** Where a transaction rests on no such agreement, the law assumes that independent prudent and diligent managers would have made one, or would assert an existing legal position, unless the taxpayer shows credibly otherwise in the single case (§ 1(4) sentence 2 AStG). | [TP.a1-4](#q-4764398d0b96aa). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 127 | 3.2 Accepted Methods LF[204, 204] | \| Hypothetical arm's length test \| Yes, where no comparable values can be found (§ 1(3) sentence 7 AStG). The ministry expects it as a rule for intangibles and rights, for a relocation of functions, and for a profit split without comparable values (no. 3.12). Recognised methods are above all income value and discounted cash flow methods (no. 3.13) \| | [TP.a1-3](#q-1a355ec9874fd1), [TP.b24-3.12](#q-8c5f79524851a6), [TP.b24-3.13](#q-2118851107a4eb). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 128 | 3.2 Accepted Methods LF[206, 206] | The ministry says the list is not closed and that a mix of methods can be right in a single case (no. 3.10). | [TP.b24-3.10](#q-6d0e28841c3e90). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 129 | 3.3 Preferred Method LF[210, 210] | - **Preferred method.** The law has no ranking with one method on top. The arm's length price is set by the method that fits best, given the comparability analysis and the comparable values that are available (§ 1(3) sentence 5 AStG). Differences between the compared transactions and the tested one are removed by proper adjustments where that is possible and makes the comparison better (§ 1(3) sentence 6 AStG). The hypothetical arm's length test applies only when no comparable values can be found. | [TP.a1-3](#q-1a355ec9874fd1). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 130 | 3.4 Range/Median LF[214, 214] | - **A range is normal.** Applying the arm's length rule as a rule gives a range of values (§ 1(3a) sentence 1 AStG). | [TP.a1-3a](#q-44322654f685a1). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 131 | 3.4 Range/Median LF[215, 215] | - **Narrowing.** The range is narrowed where differences in comparability remain. If the values themselves give no clue how to narrow, the quarter of the lowest values and the quarter of the highest values are left out (§ 1(3a) sentences 2 and 3 AStG). The ministry calls this the interquartile method (no. 3.30 of the 2024 administrative principles). | [TP.a1-3a](#q-44322654f685a1), [TP.b24-3.30](#q-ef14478fc0bc41). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 132 | 3.4 Range/Median LF[216, 216] | - **Range/median adjustment.** If the value the taxpayer used lies outside the range, or outside the narrowed range, the median counts, unless the taxpayer shows credibly that another value inside the range fits the arm's length rule better (§ 1(3a) sentence 4 AStG). This is what the law prescribes, not a choice of the tax office. | [TP.a1-3a](#q-44322654f685a1). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 133 | 3.4 Range/Median LF[217, 217] | - **Hypothetical test.** The lowest price of the provider and the highest price of the recipient as a rule give an agreement range. The mean of that range is used, unless the taxpayer shows credibly that another value in it is at arm's length (§ 1(3a) sentences 5 and 6 AStG). | [TP.a1-3a](#q-44322654f685a1). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 134 | 3.4 Range/Median LF[218, 218] | - **No records, worse range.** Where records are missing or unusable and the tax office must estimate, a range can be used in full against the taxpayer. See Section 6. | [TP.ao162-3](#q-a562a55f778f17). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

The law does not name the presumption for this case. | [TP.ao162-3](#q-a562a55f778f17). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 188 | Section 7: Advance Pricing Agreements (APA) LF[299, 299] | \| Who can apply \| A person entitled to treaty benefits. The BZSt names a business resident in Germany, or a business with a German permanent establishment that is resident in a treaty state. As a rule the foreign side should apply to its own authority at the same time. A partnership as a rule cannot apply itself, unless it has opted for corporate taxation. Where it has not opted, as a rule each partner who is resident in one of the treaty states is the person entitled to treaty benefits (decree, no. 1.8). Several persons whose case can only be judged in one way must apply together (§ 89a(1) AO) \| | [TP.apa-1](#q-8aec153ef14003), [TP.bapa-1.8](#q-57ef5928fcf823), [TP.service](#q-cef4faaefdcba2). | 214 | Section 8: Safe Harbours LF[336, 336] | \| Low-value intra-group services \| The price is as a rule set by the cost plus method. A cost mark-up of five percent (the paper prints it in words) can as a rule be seen as arm's length, if the group can show that it applies it uniformly in practice (no. 3.74). Covered: supporting services that are not the group's main business with third parties, use or create no unique and valuable intangibles, and carry no significant risk. Examples: bookkeeping, preparing tax returns, staff recruitment (no. 3.75 and 3.76). Never covered: research and development, manufacturing and production, sales, marketing and distribution (no. 3.77) \| | [TP.b24-3.74](#q-e5286491fb0a98), [TP.b24-3.75](#q-7caa03f9ef6467), [TP.b24-3.76](#q-03cf2cfb5ab507), [TP.b24-3.77](#q-b01562fc74a93b). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |
| 215 | Section 8: Safe Harbours LF[337, 337] | \| Interest rates \| No safe harbour rate. Under § 1(3d) AStG interest paid to a related party abroad is not at arm's length so far as the rate is above the rate at which the business could borrow from third parties on the rating of the whole group, unless it is proved in the single case that a rating derived from the group rating is at arm's length. The expense is also not at arm's length if the taxpayer cannot show credibly that it could have serviced the debt for the whole term from the start, and that it needed the money and used it for the purpose of the business. The ministry adds: the group's credit standing counts, unless the borrower's own is better (no. 3.133). Where the group has no rating, the rating of its top company can be used. Where that company has none either, the ministry accepts, for simplicity, a group rating worked out as at the loan date from the group's financing costs with third parties. It also accepts a credit analysis that the Deutsche Bundesbank made for the group as at the contract date (no. 3.136) \| | [TP.a1-3d](#q-7e20c555abcc3a), [TP.b24-3.133](#q-74126487147d7c), [TP.b24-3.136](#q-ee8f372a71cb09). | 244 | The method, step by step LF[383, 383] | 1. Find the covered dealings. List every business relation with a related person abroad within § 1(2) and (4) AStG, and every dealing between the business and a permanent establishment in another state within § 1(5) AStG. § 1 AStG is about business relations abroad. Dealings between two German related parties follow other rules (see the refer list). https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/astg/__1.html | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe), [TP.a1-2](#q-19d9214022907c), [TP.a1-4](#q-4764398d0b96aa), [TP.a1-5](#q-b0f5433a764e8f). | 245 | The method, step by step LF[384, 384] | 2. Check the result against the range and median rules of § 1(3a) AStG. https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/astg/__1.html | [TP.a1-3](#q-1a355ec9874fd1), [TP.a1-3a](#q-44322654f685a1). | 254 | Example 1: eligible low-value support service LF[398, 398] | These are fictional 2026 inputs. A German group service company supplies bookkeeping support to its foreign related group company. The recipient receives an independently established business benefit and would pay for the service or perform it itself. The service is supportive, is not the group's principal external business, neither uses nor creates unique or valuable intangibles, and neither assumes nor controls significant risks nor creates them. It is not manufacturing, R&D, sales, marketing or distribution. The eligible service cost base has been established at EUR 100,000, with no pass-through costs or excluded shareholder/duplicated activity in this example. The multinational group demonstrably applies the 5% mark-up consistently in practice. These facts satisfy the selected administrative low-value-service route; a bookkeeping label alone does not establish eligibility. Under BMF administrative principles of 12 December 2024, paragraphs 3.74–3.77, the illustrative price is EUR 100,000 + EUR 5,000 = EUR 105,000. [Official BMF text](https://www.bundesfinanzministerium.de/Content/DE/Downloads/BMF_Schreiben/Internationales_Steuerrecht/Allgemeine_Informationen/2024-12-12-vwg-verrechnungspreise-2024.pdf?__blob=publicationFile&v=1). | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe), [TP.b24-3.74](#q-e5286491fb0a98), [TP.b24-3.75](#q-7caa03f9ef6467), [TP.b24-3.76](#q-03cf2cfb5ab507), [TP.b24-3.77](#q-b01562fc74a93b). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 266 | Example 2: matched independent CUP sale and German income correction LF[414, 414] | These are fictional 2026 inputs, not a benchmark for a real client. A German company owns 60% of its foreign customer and sells it 1,000 identical units at EUR 100 per unit. The same German seller has a genuinely comparable independent sale at EUR 120 per unit. The example stipulates matched timing, market, currency, quantities, functions, risks, assets, delivery, payment, warranty and other economically material terms, with no adjustment required. The comparability study establishes that this CUP is the best-fitting method on these facts. The independent price is an example input; it is not a statutory price or an automatic preference for CUP. The related-party sale reduces German income, so § 1(1) AStG restores the income that would have arisen at arm's length. Related-party revenue is EUR 100,000, arm's-length revenue EUR 120,000, and the income increase EUR 20,000. This computes the income correction only, not corporation tax, trade tax, customs, VAT or a foreign corresponding adjustment. [AStG § 1(1)–(3)](https://www.gesetze-im-internet.de/astg/__1.html); [BMF paragraph 3.9](https://www.bundesfinanzministerium.de/Content/DE/Downloads/BMF_Schreiben/Internationales_Steuerrecht/Allgemeine_Informationen/2024-12-12-vwg-verrechnungspreise-2024.pdf?__blob=publicationFile&v=1). | [TP.a1-1](#q-871217aa4992fe), [TP.a1-2](#q-19d9214022907c), [TP.a1-3](#q-1a355ec9874fd1), [TP.b24-3.9](#q-284c56fa4ab94a). Exact prior current unit with rebound native coordinates |

| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF19 | How Germany prices and documents dealings between a business and its related parties abroad: the arm's length rule, the records a business must keep, the master file, the country-by-country report, what must be handed over in a tax audit and when, the surcharges, and advance pricing procedures. It is for German companies, partnerships and permanent establishments that deal with related parties abroad, and for their advisers. Figures are for tax year 2026. The statute figures are read from the consolidated federal law pages as they stood in September 2026. Four finance ministry papers are also used, each dated another year: the administrative principles on transfer pricing of 12 December 2024, the information sheet on the transaction matrix of 2 April 2025, the application decree on advance pricing procedures of 26 June 2024, and the administrative principles on documentation duties of 3 December 2020. The 2020 paper is older than the changes that apply from 1 January 2025. This Guide uses it only for points that the later law did not change, and names it each time. | Exact wording retained: Transfer pricing in Germany (Verrechnungspreise), currentLF19 R61: ordinary annual deadline is qualified by the justified-expectation/no-fault one-month-from-knowledge catch-up, including specified German PE and unavailable-report notice alternative. |
| 62b7639a5a68324a83a96bb0190fff8b7c76f8c5 LF23 | Version 1.0 of this Guide, refreshed against the official pages for 2026. | Presentation version updated to1.2; currentLF23, no substantive rule dropped  |
